# Implementation status

Core C0–C6 and the owner-authorized foundation, authentication, data-integrity, UI, Calendar, Food, Exercise, Admin and Fitness workouts, progress, Training Programs and general activity-model passes, plus Everyday Food, the shared domain Calendar day panel, Everyday Tasks and the Assistant workspace/quick-chat preview, are complete. The owner-authorized Admin pass adds permanent account types, account management, aggregate statistics, operational health and an immutable action log. Remaining work in this authorized scope: none. Genuine blockers: none. CI remains removed; checks run locally. No AI integration, external integrations, native app, social features, notification delivery or background jobs were added.

## Templates deferred from first release, complete (2026-10-29)

Preview scope retains the full implementation and stored templates. Kept the shared copy service and ordinary Calendar clipboard/copy/undo routes available in core; no data or schema changes.
- Checks passed: four PostgreSQL release-boundary cases, including every disabled template read/write family, retained preview name/version and working core copying; nine first-release browser cases across desktop, phone and tablet, including absent Templates entry/endpoints; two desktop/phone preview template-copy journeys, including retry and persistent undo/redo. Full workspace typechecks, zero-warning lint/architecture boundaries, repository formatting and diff whitespace passed. The initial sandboxed integration attempt could not connect to the local test database (EPERM); the authorized local rerun passed.
- Updated FEATURES, scope, UI standards, README and environment examples. No commit, push or deployment.

One pre-existing Day fixture still expected a finished scheduled workout to be highlighted as Next; corrected it to assert completion in Coming up with no Next emphasis, then reran the full affected workflow successfully. ## Extensible Calendar domain scheduling, complete (2026-10-29)

- Added a small source registry at Calendar’s composition boundary. The Add menu offers Add plan and registered domain actions; Fitness is the first adapter. Shared workspace types pass local date/timezone and optional minute/duration hints to a domain-owned scheduler and receive a resulting local date plus feedback. The result does not force timed durations onto future point/date-only domains. A shared source picker handles presentation, search input, scrollable choices, pagination, loading/empty/error states and dismissal; adapters retain their own eligible records, hooks and canonical writes. No inactive-domain placeholders or generic mutation API were introduced.
- Fitness searches ready reusable workouts using its existing query hook, excludes drafts, and reuses ScheduleWorkout and the existing public scheduling endpoint. Exact Calendar time selections override automatic rounding; without a time selection, the existing next-quarter-hour behavior applies. Optional duration hints preserve elapsed time. The shared scheduling form shows workout notes, and Calendar can return to the retained search through Change workout with dirty-timing confirmation. Success shows a Calendar toast and reveals the reserved date in the same view. The original reusable workout and sessions stay unchanged; the scheduled snapshot and linked block remain canonical.
- Checks passed: eight workout-scheduling unit tests including exact selected minute, midnight and duration hints; web typecheck; full zero-warning lint and architecture boundaries; 12 relevant browser cases across desktop/320px phone with targeted reruns. New end-to-end coverage verifies search/pagination, excluded drafts, exact 09:05 placement, notes, retained search, dirty change confirmation, lost-response same-key retry with one reservation, original revision preservation, no recorded workout creation and the linked Start action. Empty/no-match/load-failure recovery creates no reservation. Existing Fitness scheduling and ordinary timed/all-day/repeating creation regressions passed through the new Add menu. Reviewed compact desktop/phone picker screenshots; integrated its single rounded focus ring into shared form-focus styling. Initial new test fixtures used generic Retry text rather than the existing Retry same change/Try again labels; corrected those selectors and reran all failures successfully.
- Updated Calendar form fixtures to choose Add plan through the shared menu. FEATURES and UI standards document the reusable pattern and shipped behavior. Final formatting and whitespace checks passed. No API/schema changes, commit, push, seeding or deployment.

## Complete scheduled-workout journey, complete (2026-10-29)

- Added a permanent isolated-account browser journey: schedule through Fitness, follow View in Calendar, start from the reserved block, change actual reps/weight, complete a set, pause with rest running, advance time and refresh, resume, skip a set, move to another exercise, complete a set with unknown weight, leave entered but unfinished values, finish, review Summary, explicitly correct results, check History and exercise records, and return to Calendar. The checks confirm unchanged reusable targets/revision, preserved planned reservation, excluded pauses, one canonical recorded-time source, and corrections reflected in exercise records. The phone run uses a 320px browser viewport.
- Screenshot review found that an early-finished scheduled workout still appeared as Next during its reserved future slot. The shared day feed now excludes finished scheduled workouts from Now/Next emphasis while retaining their planned cards and time, labelled Workout completed. An owner-scoped repository join projects completion from the scheduled plan’s linked session through the existing public day-links service/contract, independently of the recorded week; other activity linked to a block does not establish workout completion. This applies in Calendar and shared domain Day panels. Reservations moved to another week still show completion. Deleting the recorded workout clears that projection while preserving planned time.
- The Calendar reservation editor now reads the linked session before choosing its workout action: Start workout when unstarted, Continue workout while active, and View workout summary when finished. Loading and read failures have status/retry feedback. Reopening a completed reservation returns to the existing summary without creating another session.
- Checks passed: complete journey 2/2 desktop/320px after the final changes; shared domain Day regressions 2/2; focused real-PostgreSQL strength suite 12/12, including cross-week completion, deletion, planned-time preservation and account isolation; full workspace typechecks, final web typecheck, full lint/boundaries and focused final ESLint, formatting and diff whitespace. Reviewed Calendar screenshots and automated accessibility/overflow checks. Initial test fixtures read the reservation before scheduling settled and supplied response-only fields to a strict update contract; corrected the harness to wait for completion and send the intended wire input. Updated FEATURES. Shared day-links responses add completedBlockIds; no database migration, production data changes, commit, push or deployment.

## Fitness secondary actions, complete (2026-10-29)

- Duplicate exercise now opens the shared creator with a captured definition, a suffixed editable name and Favourite off. No write occurs until Create exercise; Cancel leaves no copy and restores source action focus. Confirmed creation opens the new custom definition. Both built-in and custom sources stay unchanged; copied instructions, equipment, muscles, measurement defaults and retained intent values use the normal owner-scoped create contract. The former duplicate API remains available. Seeded creation uses the existing validation, discard guard and same-request recovery; refreshing an uncertain copy creation on a standalone exercise route restores the creator and retries the original request rather than updating the source or creating another copy.
- Exercise Hide/Show and Archive/Restore now give named success toasts with Undo, using explicit desired values and the confirmed exercise revision. Immediate cache updates keep subsequent actions current; normal service revision checks still reject stale Undo attempts. Pending/uncertain detail mutations and favourites protect in-app navigation. Successful deletion waits for its command to settle before invoking contextual Back so its own pending guard cannot trap the return. Referenced custom exercises show disabled Delete with a visible reason and Archive guidance; backend eligibility and built-in restrictions are unchanged.
- Reusable-workout menu, confirmation and action consistently say Delete reusable workout; drafts retain Delete draft. Save as new workout confirms Saved “[title]” to My workouts and retains Edit workout. It still copies planned targets, preserves the current session and prevents repeated identical copies.
- Checks passed: full zero-warning lint and boundary checks, web typecheck, eight new desktop/phone secondary-action browser checks and six existing exercise/summary regression flows. Coverage includes copy cancellation, built-in copy defaults, dropped successful copy response and refresh/same-key retry without duplication, visibility Undo, blocked navigation during a held request, unavailable referenced deletion, cancelled/confirmed unused deletion with correct return, reusable deletion wording, named reusable-copy feedback, preserved session revision and planned targets, and accessibility. Initial fixture failures used an incorrect catalogue key and removed a held route before its handler completed; corrected both. Accessibility checks now wait for the toast action to be enabled and its entrance animation to finish before measuring contrast. Formatting and diff whitespace passed. Updated FEATURES and removed the addressed secondary-action notes. No API/schema changes, commit, push or deployment.

## Shared styled confirmations and navigation guards, complete (2026-10-29)

- Promoted the existing Fitness confirmation presentation to a shared UI component and added asynchronous confirmation/text-entry providers. Replaced all remaining native confirmation calls in Tasks, Food, measurements, routines, categories and Calendar, including template rename prompts. ESLint now rejects native alert/confirm/prompt globals and window/globalThis properties in web source. Existing command payloads, revision checks and domain services remain unchanged.
- Replaced the synchronous unsaved-change guard with a registered asynchronous guard for ordinary links, guarded imperative navigation and browser Back/Forward. It uses Discard changes / Keep editing, preserves cancelled drafts and history entries, replays accepted links without dropping their callbacks or confirming twice, and blocks pending/uncertain writes. Correct results invokes its canonical discard/reset before leaving, so Training navigation cannot recover a supposedly discarded correction later. Added tested history-entry restoration for browsers without the Navigation API. Removed beforeunload registration at the owner’s explicit request: refresh/tab close has no native warning; existing recovery still applies where available, while non-recovering forms may lose unsaved edits.
- Fixed modal layering exposed by the broader check: Assistant preview no longer adopts confirmation dialogs as editor hosts, keeping the retained popup beneath confirmations and restoring cancelled navigation correctly. Template text entry uses styled controls without native constraint-validation bubbles. Updated FEATURES/UI standards and older task/Food/Assistant test interactions.
- Checks passed: full zero-warning workspace lint and boundaries; web typecheck; isolated production web build; formatting and whitespace. Twenty desktop/phone browser flows passed together across shared confirmation/navigation, summary/correction/repeat, Tasks and Food; focused nested Assistant/editor cancellation flows passed 2/2 after the layering fix. Coverage includes Training and Back/Forward cancellation/acceptance through both Navigation API and simulated history fallback, discarded-draft clearing, refresh recovery without beforeunload, zero native dialogs, nested modal discard, template rename/delete/cancel, imperative Day-panel navigation, pending-save blocking, retry/stale edits, accessibility and screenshot review. Initial new tests needed an explicit navigation URL wait and the correct Open Calendar link role; corrected these before the passing run. No API, schema, commit or deployment changes.

## Persistent Live workout Pause/Resume, complete (2026-10-28)

- Chose to exclude pauses from workout duration. Pause/Resume sits beside the status in the sticky Live area, freezes workout/rest timers, disables live logging until resumed and persists ordered pause intervals through the existing revision/idempotency/draft-recovery/save flow. Finishing while paused closes the final interval. Rest retains its origin and only gains pauses overlapping that rest, preserving its remaining duration after refresh. Timers freeze at the saved transition rather than a stale display tick, and Resume cannot briefly move time backwards.
- Added shared duration/range helpers and pause validation. Summary, History, comparisons and Fitness weekly totals use workout time including rest but excluding pauses. Timing corrections clip pauses to valid corrected bounds while retaining them during invalid edits. Summary shows paused duration in a compact full-width note rather than expanding the primary metric on phones. Calendar keeps real start/finish bounds and one unique source-linked record; recorded/category totals exclude paused intervals with correct range clipping, and automatic-entry details explain the exclusion. Routes remain thin and private records retain owner scope.
- Added migration 0038 for session pause intervals and source-record exclusions; applied it to the isolated test database and verified local development database only, with a local-host/non-production guard. No production operations, reset, seeding, commit, push or deployment. Updated FEATURES/UI standards and removed the pending Pause note.
- Direct-discard verification exposed an existing active-session redirect to History. Discard now returns to Training and clears local rest, while deleting finished sessions retains contextual History navigation and reusable workouts remain intact.
- Checks passed: full workspace typecheck; 170 unit tests across 41 files and six boundary tests; focused zero-warning ESLint, boundary validation, formatting and whitespace. Across focused integration runs, 25 distinct Fitness strength/progress/pause and planning tests passed, including private access, malformed/overlapping/open-pause rejection, persisted state, same-intent retry, matching Fitness/Calendar totals, corrections and deletion. Across browser runs, fourteen distinct desktop/phone Live/History/pause flows passed; the final Pause/History run passed 4/4 and the final summary-presentation run passed 2/2. New coverage verifies frozen timers/logging, reload, resume, multiple pauses, finish while paused, direct discard and retained reusable workouts, accessibility and 320px overflow. Reviewed desktop/phone paused and narrow summary screenshots. Corrected test import, mock-clock hydration and duplicated Calendar/status locator issues during verification; tests also identified the real freeze-tick and discard-navigation fixes.

## First-release domain boundary, complete (2026-10-27)

- Added a shared core/preview release policy. Core is the default for web and API: main navigation exposes Calendar, Fitness and Tasks; Food and Assistant direct pages return not found, and the Assistant provider/navigation/launcher do not mount. Food, preparation, meal-log, nutrition, hydration, body-weight and legacy food-bearing dashboard endpoints are not registered in core scope. Food/water/weight Calendar providers and measurement quick tools/grid shortcuts are disabled there.

Updated FEATURES in place to distinguish default release behaviour from retained preview functionality. This cleanup does not approve public deployment or implement Tasks/security readiness work.
- Existing API integration fixtures explicitly use preview unless a test requests core. The retained-feature Playwright suite also explicitly starts preview services; a separate `playwright.release.config.ts` / `pnpm test:e2e:release` verifies the default core boundary. No production data was seeded or deleted and no deployment occurred.

Core desktop/phone browser checks passed 2/2, covering three-domain navigation, missing Assistant/measurement entry points, blocked direct pages/APIs, working Fitness navigation/Tasks capture, accessibility and overflow. Preview desktop/phone checks passed 6/6 for retained Food/recipes/meals, Assistant round-trip with workout recovery, and Calendar quick tools. ## Tasks release-scope review, complete (2026-10-27)

- Reviewed Tasks capture/list views, project organization, task/time forms, repeating occurrences, Calendar reservations and actual-time accounting, validation, revision/ownership checks and pending-save handling. Reviewed desktop/phone captures. No product changes were made; release-review findings are recorded under Tasks Needs work in FEATURES.
- Recommendation: Tasks is a viable first-release domain after a focused reliability/consistency pass. Existing capture, project, recurrence and Calendar integration work; a large redesign or new task features are not required to assess inclusion. This is a scope recommendation, not public deployment/security approval.
- Required reliability work: task/project create/update, quick capture and schedule/time commands use in-memory retry intent without persisted refresh recovery. TaskDialog's navigation guard omits the busy blocking argument, so confirming navigation can abandon an unresolved operation even though closing the dialog itself is blocked. This leaves a similar retry/recovery risk to the Fitness issue just fixed; it was identified by source review, not a new fault-injection browser probe.
- Consistency/accessibility work: native browser confirmation prompts remain throughout Tasks, and time-entry validation uses a general alert rather than field-specific invalid/error association and focus. Ordinary unsaved-draft loss on refresh is already documented separately from unresolved writes.
- Checks passed: 3 recurrence unit tests, 7 Tasks API integration tests, 6 desktop/phone browser scenarios and focused Tasks ESLint. Browser coverage includes capture, projects, scheduling, time correction, repetition/history, validation, stale edits, same-key retry and quick-capture day/navigation protection. The existing browser suite includes accessibility checking in the main workflow; it does not establish complete accessibility or refresh recovery.

## Fitness audit fixes, complete (2026-10-27)

- Workout creation/editing now persists each save's payload, original revision, copy intent and request key under an owner/workout-scoped key. Refresh restores Retry same change and blocks a second save until the original outcome is confirmed. Replaying a committed create produces one workout; replaying an update uses its original revision without updating twice. Storage failure blocks sending an unrecoverable save. Shared command recovery now runs after hydration and before interaction, avoiding mismatched server/client recovery UI.
- History sorting and cursor boundaries now use startedAt plus ID, while wire createdAt remains the actual creation timestamp. Timing corrections reposition sessions. Added integration coverage for backdated entries, tied dates across pages, corrected dates and owner isolation.
- Removed the empty final-Live handoff target and gave actual handoff groups a supported role; final-set focus can fall back to Finish workout. Recorded correction summaries now use each completed set's selected display units, with unit alignment retained when skipped/unfinished sets are filtered out. Added mixed-unit and browser coverage.
- Updated Progress tests for explicit Save corrections and expanded Live exercises; updated Program lifecycle assertions for the expanded exercise and current Summary heading. Their later correction/deletion, repeat/recall, completion, pause/resume and retained-session assertions now execute and pass.
- Updated FEATURES and removed all five addressed audit notes. Relevant ESLint, workspace typechecks, boundaries, formatting and whitespace checks passed. Fitness unit tests passed 53/53 across 12 files. Four API integration files passed 29/29 tests. The final targeted desktop/phone browser run passed 12/12 cases, including original-key/payload/revision replay after refresh, no duplicates or hydration errors, final-set accessibility and finish failure/retry, per-set unit headers, saved corrections/deletions and the full Program lifecycle. Final-set focus follow-up passed 2/2 desktop/phone checks.
- Initial checks caught a new expectation using three decimal places where the established load formatter uses two, the Program test's remaining old review label, and the recovered-command hydration mismatch. Corrected these and reran the affected checks successfully.

## Fitness domain audit, review complete (2026-10-27)

- Reviewed the Fitness workspace/library, custom and built-in definitions, reusable-workout editor/recovery, Live session state/rest/saving, summary/corrections, History/progress, Programs, scheduling/Calendar integration, contracts, domain services and owner-scoped repositories. No product implementation changes were made; findings are recorded under Fitness Needs work in FEATURES.
- Confirmed a new-workout recovery defect with a synthetic browser probe: server save succeeded, response was aborted, refresh restored the local draft but no pending-command key, and a second Save draft created a second workout (one saved record became two; request keys differed). WorkoutBuilder uses an unpersisted useCommand, unlike Program saving and scheduling. In-page retry remains keyed; refresh is the failing case.
- Confirmed History ordering with synthetic sessions: an October 20 workout created after an October 26 workout appeared first because listing/cursors use createdAt. Confirmed correction-unit mismatch: changing the set to lb displayed 44.09 lb while the recorded header still showed 20 kg; the planned reference correctly used lb.
- Confirmed an accessibility regression after completing the final Live set: the empty fitness-live-handoff div retains tabindex and aria-label without a supported role. The existing Axe check reports aria-prohibited-attr on desktop and phone.
- Broad browser audit: 28/34 passed across desktop and phone. Six failures comprised the same three cases on each device: the actual final-Live accessibility defect, a Progress test still waiting for autosave instead of Save corrections, and a Program lifecycle test waiting for Expand while its first Live exercise is already expanded. The latter two are stale test assumptions, not evidence of broken progress or Program APIs. Their later assertions remain unverified in this run.
- Fitness unit tests: 12 files / 52 tests passed. API integration audit: 7 files / 39 tests passed (exercise/catalog, general Fitness, strength, progress, Programs and authorization). Focused Fitness/API ESLint, workspace typechecks and boundary checks passed. Synthetic browser probes additionally verified the duplicate-save, date ordering and local-unit cases. No owner data was used for probes; no commits, deployment or production seeding occurred.
- Priorities: preserve pending workout-save intent across refresh first, resolve the final-set accessibility failure, then align History chronology and correction display units; repair the stale browser expectations so downstream progress/Program assertions execute again. Existing intentional limitations (placeholder illustrations, archived-exercise reuse rules and local-only recovery) were kept separate from defects.

Existing service/routes/client retain their contract boundary; no schema migration.
- The chooser results scroll within a viewport-bounded area while Add and the separate Create new workout/Cancel controls remain outside it. Selection persists across filtering and pagination, with the destination named in the Add button. Search changes reset the list scroll position; opening the chooser starts with empty search and no selection. Updated FEATURES and UI standards.
- Workspace typechecks, focused zero-warning ESLint, boundaries, formatting and diff whitespace passed. Seven Fitness PostgreSQL integration tests passed, including new search pagination, case/whitespace normalization, literal percent/underscore/backslash, empty results, length validation and account isolation. The initial length-validation test expected 400; corrected it to the existing 422 API convention. Six desktop/phone browser scenarios passed for overview, add flow and search, including unloaded results, independent search, retained selections, bounded scrolling, stationary Add after pagination and accessibility. Two search scenarios passed again for viewport captures; reviewed phone and desktop layout captures. No commit or deployment.

## Fitness UX consistency pass, complete

- Implemented the owner-authorized second Fitness pass. Finished workouts now open a compact read-only review with date/time, elapsed duration, completion counts, notes and expandable actual-result summaries. Correct results explicitly opens the existing correction controls; Done correcting returns after saving. Unknown, zero, skipped and unfinished results remain distinct, and opening a review does not write. Repeat stays prominent, while comparison and secondary actions sit below the results. Session editing visibly identifies its scope.
- Program forms now use numbered workout steps, shared notes controls, field-level validation and a pinned save bar. Account/tab-local recovery retains incomplete fields and ordering, announces restoration with the standard toast and exposes a quiet discard action. Conflicting revisions block overwriting; interrupted saves retain their original input, revision and request key through refresh. The editor mounts after hydration so recovered requests do not produce server/client markup mismatches. Phone Assistant placement clears the save controls.
- Duration targets and actual results use labelled minutes/seconds inputs throughout basic, advanced and live editors. Unused components remain blank, focus selects existing digits, and summaries, history and comparisons use readable durations. Duration columns receive room for both components while preserving other set-field alignment, horizontal scrolling and keyboard order. Shared Fitness dialogs replace native confirmations for workout/exercise deletion, discarding edited schedules/programs, partial finishing and reloading local session edits. Modal close/retry controls explicitly avoid form submission.
- Validation: root lint and domain/persistence boundaries, workspace typechecks, 47 focused Fitness/measurement unit tests, changed-file formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. Across the final targeted browser runs, 48 distinct desktop/phone scenarios passed: creator layout and keyboard navigation, measurement overrides and copying, live progression and undo, invalid fields/times/rest, history and corrections, program operations, scheduling, durable recovery, queued writes, lost responses, protected conflicts and read-only review. The last focused run passed all 8 creator/review/program checks; accessibility scans passed in the exercised flows. Desktop/phone review, program and duration-layout captures were inspected.
- Initial browser runs exposed duration-label alignment and stale one-field/tab-order expectations; corrected the layout and tests. Interrupted program creation exposed a hydration mismatch; the final recovery scenarios assert no browser page errors and pass. Fixed unchanged-draft comparison across object key order. Updated FEATURES and UI standards, removing the addressed review/program/duration gaps while retaining deferred catalogue, visual assets and broader live-experience notes. No API/schema changes, commit, push or deployment in this pass. This authorized pass stops here.

## Fitness functional reliability pass, complete

- Implemented the owner-authorized functional pass from the Fitness audit. Recorded, skipped and deliberately edited sets require confirmation before structural removal; Undo removal restores results even after saving, with fresh persisted identities for removed rows. Typed basic set counts commit on blur rather than truncating between keystrokes. A durable `resultsEdited` flag preserves deliberately cleared actuals across saving/refresh; new/repeated sessions reset it.
- Session saves retain their submitted payload, revision and request key in the account/session draft. Users can continue editing during a known in-flight save; reconciliation maps assigned IDs by occurrence and queues later edits without replacing them with the older response. Uncertain writes block further edits until same-key retry, including after refresh. Local-storage failures warn and guard navigation. Shared navigation protection now covers same-document Back/Forward through the Navigation API, in addition to links, domain navigation and unload protection.
- Workout and program scheduling protect pending/uncertain dialogs and navigation, retain the original payload/revision/key through refresh, display the recovered reviewed times and retry the same operation. These schedules are not submitted if recovery storage cannot retain the pending request. Training lists every active session with pagination; starting another workout, program session or repeat offers existing workouts to resume. Rest timers identify the exercise and retain their deadline per account/session across editor switches, navigation and refresh in the same tab.
- Session and program validation associates errors with the affected fields and focuses an actionable error when saving. Actual-field errors are retained after reload. Tooltips now portal inside the nearest dialog or page landmark, resolving the creator landmark violation while retaining shared tooltip behavior. This pass keeps the existing layouts and finished-workout correction form; the larger UX pass remains separate.
- Exercise history defaults to all recorded measurements, with available combinations listed for filtered statistics and records. Set tables respect each captured selection and distinguish unselected values from unknown values. Exact filtered recall remains available. Workout comparisons match exercise/measurement signatures independently of overall exercise order and align repeated identical occurrences in occurrence order; owner scoping and selected-measurement metrics remain intact.
- Added migration `0035_lazy_juggernaut.sql` for the results-edited flag and generated its schema snapshot. The additive migration succeeded against the verified local development database and the isolated test database. No data reset, backfill, production operation, dependency or service was added.
- Validation: root lint (including domain/persistence boundaries), workspace typechecks, 42 focused Fitness/measurement unit tests and 25 distinct Fitness/program API integration tests passed. Across the final targeted browser runs, 42 distinct desktop/phone scenarios passed, covering creator and exercise editing, live training/navigation, recorded removal/undo, queued saves and lost responses, recovered scheduling, all active workouts, mixed-measurement history, invalid fields/times/rest, progress and program workflows. Accessibility scans passed in the exercised flows. Desktop history and phone validation renders were inspected. Changed-file Prettier and diff whitespace checks passed.
- Updated stale API expectations for valid independent measurements and selection-aware metrics, plus older exercise-picker, live completion, history-filter and rest-editor browser selectors. The first broader run exposed the retired raw-seconds rest test; it now injects an invalid recovery draft and waits for the current editor before testing correction. A new Back-navigation probe exposed the limits of the initial popstate guard; the Navigation API guard passed the final desktop/phone checks. Updated FEATURES and UI standards, retaining the owner’s outstanding UX and feature notes. No commit, push or deployment. This authorized pass stops here.

## Fitness domain audit, complete

- Audited Training, reusable workout creation/editing, library/custom exercises, live setup/logging, scheduling, programs, review, exercise history/records, shared conventions and accessibility against current contracts, services and UI standards. Inspected desktop and phone renders. This was a review only: no application behavior changed. Added practical gaps under Fitness Needs work in FEATURES; these notes do not authorize implementation.
- Browser probes reproduced completed results being removed when reducing live set count without confirmation/undo; all-cleared actual results reverting to offered targets after reload; dismissal of an uncertain successful schedule losing its retry action; multiple active sessions with only one Training continuation card; exact measurement combinations hiding existing exercise history; and lost unsaved session edits when local storage fails and the user navigates before autosave. Session setup also reports a generic validation error without marking/associating the affected field.
- UX priorities: protect recorded results and unresolved writes, provide a compact finished-workout summary with explicit correction, expose available history without requiring remembered measurement combinations, clarify reusable-workout versus current-session scope, and bring program forms, confirmations and duration formats into the same conventions. Finished review still exposes the old full correction form; live autosaves disable the whole form while pending. Rest timers remain tab-local and clear on refresh/edit-mode changes. These are audit findings/recommendations, not implemented changes.
- All 37 focused Fitness/measurement unit tests, web typecheck and boundary checks passed. Of 22 Fitness/general/progress/program API integration tests, 19 passed. Three failures reflect stale expectations: rejecting now-valid distance-only tracking, including an unselected distance value in comparison metrics, and expecting a different personal-record array order. No application changes or test expectation changes were made to hide these failures.
- Across desktop and phone, 15 of 18 existing browser scenarios passed: live progression/reordering/undo, direct help return and session editing, recovery, exercise records/weekly totals, read-error retries and program conflict/retry behavior. Two exercise-editor runs timed out at the retired creator Add exercise button; the desktop creation-preview run reported an Axe region best-practice violation for the portalled Remove set tooltip outside landmarks. The phone creator scenario passed. Other scanned audit/program/history/review views had no Axe violations; this does not substitute for real-device or screen-reader testing.
- Six targeted browser probes reproduced the issues on both viewports. The follow-up probe initially matched Next's route-announcer alert as well as the validation alert; narrowing that test selector produced two passing reruns. Temporary probes were removed from the repository and retained under `/tmp/168hours-fitness-audit` with the original regression failure contexts; visual captures are `/tmp/fitness-audit-*.png`. Documentation formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. No commit, deployment or production data changes.

## Fitness Training Programs, complete

- Added program creation/editing with named ordered reusable-workout steps, notes, a start date, optional preferred weekdays or a flexible rotation, keyboard/touch reordering, pause/resume and active/paused/all lists. Fitness’s overview surfaces active programs, and sessions link back to their program. Programs repeat after the final step; there is no bulk scheduling or automatic load/repetition progression.
- The next workout supports start/continue, explicit Calendar scheduling, moving the same reservation, and skip with confirmation. Prepared copies preserve exercise/target snapshots. Finishing the canonical linked session advances the sequence whether started in Fitness or Calendar. Skipping removes its unstarted copy and reserved time; in-progress sessions must be finished or discarded first. History exposes finished/skipped counts, cycle/step, actual session/skip dates versus planned dates and session links.
- Added shared contracts/client calls, thin routes, canonical Exercise service/repository and migration 0028 for programs/occurrences, owner constraints, unique step sequences and revision triggers. Parent locks, stable source-lock ordering, expected sequences, block revisions and idempotent receipts prevent duplicate starts/skips and stale reschedules. Missing/draft sources remain visible and replaceable/skippable. Calendar deletion/undo reconnects unchanged copies and refuses restoration after replacement or skipping. Program deletion preserves independent sessions/plans/Calendar entries. Latest-session deletion returns its step to ready; older deletion does not rewind later steps.
- Forms preserve entered values on errors, warn before discarding unsaved changes, freeze the edited revision and retain request keys for uncertain retries. A nullable session-program lookup uses an object envelope to distinguish “no program” from an unreadable response. Updated FEATURES, scope, architecture and UI standards. Applied the migration successfully to both local test and verified development databases; no reset, seed or production changes.

- All 20 desktop/phone browser flows passed across programs, Strength, Progress and recovery, including automated accessibility. Initial new browser fixtures were corrected to enter an actual measurement before completing a set and to distinguish the application’s error from Next’s route announcer. ## Compact workout exercise/set cards, complete

- Replaced large added-exercise cards with compact headers, name-based exercise help, visible remove controls, six-dot handles and a shared-column planned-set table. Handle dragging supports mouse/touch, local move previews, insertion indicators, edge scrolling and Escape/pointer-cancel cleanup. ## Selector removal and single-click dismissal, complete

- Exercise equipment/muscle chip removal now closes options without returning pointer focus to search. Keyboard removal focuses a surviving chip, or the search field with its popup suppressed when the last chip is removed. Selecting an option also restores search focus without accidentally reopening it on touch.
- Added one shared pointer-dismissal hook for exercise multi-selects, library filter searches, workout choice and History status. It checks the actual input/associated label, options and trigger rather than a broad layout wrapper, closes on nearby blank-space clicks and releases focus without cancelling the outside action. It also works while a popup is already closed. Single-select menus now float instead of shifting surrounding controls on dismissal; Escape works from their trigger as well as their search. Updated FEATURES and UI standards.
- Verification: web typecheck, changed-file ESLint, formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. Final desktop/phone Fitness workspace flows passed, including pointer/touch removal for equipment and muscles in creation, edit removal, native touch option selection, no popup reopening, keyboard removal through the last chip, precise near-field one-click dismissal, native touch outside dismissal, workout selector dismissal, History selection and accessibility. Both final flows passed. No backend changes or commits.

## Unified timed/all-day plan editor and conversion, complete

- Owner-authorized scope: timed and all-day are two modes of the same plan. Creation and editing use the shared compact form with an All day toggle. Multi-day conversion produces one continuous timed plan with editable start/end dates and times, including the intervening nights. Templates and other domains were not expanded.
- Saving a mode change replaces the source while retaining its UUID, creation date, title, notes and references. Unsaved toggles preserve entered time fields; cancelling does not write. Timed-to-all-day conversion covers occupied local dates and excludes an end exactly at midnight. All-day-to-timed conversion carries the first/last dates into an editable range. The separate copy action is explicitly named Create timed copy….
- Added an owner-scoped conversion contract, typed client and thin route backed by the existing Calendar series service. Transactions move the same plan identity between existing persistence tables. Canonical block deletion receipts preserve recorded activity and support restoring its links. No migration or data reset was needed.
- Individual repeating conversions remain attached to their original series as exceptions. Following/all scopes update the rule and preserve other individual edits by default; explicit replacement applies the rule's mode. Normal scoped edits also handle occurrences whose mode differs from the rule. Missing clock times that would silently remove converted occurrences are rejected atomically. Source/parent revisions, owner-scoped references, timezone checks and original-key retries remain enforced.
- Mode changes record one undo/redo action using reversible before/after snapshots, including mixed-mode occurrences and following-series splits. History retains them across Calendar navigation and refresh. New browser coverage checks true replacement, one continuous range, cancellation, toggle round trips, lost-success retries, repetition scopes, consecutive undo/redo and editor accessibility on desktop and phone.
- Updated `docs/FEATURES.md` in place and preserved the requested Move… improvement note. Recorded the existing activity-link model's limit under All-day plans → Needs work: conversion to all-day retains recorded records/times but detaches their plan links; Undo restores the links, while a new conversion back does not reconnect them. The old all-day-specific form is removed; the remaining date-only form handles Unscheduled plans.
- Verification: workspace typechecks, zero-warning lint/import boundaries, 68 unit tests and six architecture tests passed. The full PostgreSQL run passed 95 of 96 cases; its only failure was a strict undo request fixture containing extra series-result fields. After correcting that fixture, all four conversion cases passed, covering owner/reference isolation, stale revisions, retry replay, recorded activity, mixed-mode overrides, scoped edits, clock-gap rejection and reversible conversion. API and webpack web production builds passed.
- Browser verification: all 50 distinct desktop/phone Calendar flows passed across the final focused runs, including four new conversion flows and eight existing repeating/deletion flows. The broader run initially passed 36 of 38 flows; two desktop pointer fixtures targeted positions underneath the sticky heading. Centering their targets corrected the fixtures, and both focused reruns passed. Workspace formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. No commit, push or deployment was performed.

## Repeating Calendar plans, complete

- Owner-authorized scope: make existing timed/all-day plans repeat; show readable rule summaries; offer this occurrence, this and following, or entire series for edits/moves/resizes/deletions; preserve individual edits by default with an explicit replace option; stop after a chosen date; support date-based all-day repetition. Daily/weekly cadence remains finite. Templates and other domains were unchanged.
- Converting a standalone plan retains its identity as the first occurrence. Following changes split future occurrences into a separate series, keeping earlier rules, future identities, individual overrides and excluded dates. Rule changes retain overrides within the new date range, including weekdays no longer in the rule. Group deletion continues to remove only selected occurrences. Stopping preserves earlier starts and full multi-day spans.
- Timed and all-day series share transaction-protected services, owner-scoped reference checks, optimistic revisions and idempotency. A scope change retains its original parent revision/key across an uncertain retry. Title-only occurrence changes do not spread an exceptional time/duration to the series. All-day recurrence uses dates across daylight-saving changes and remains separate from allocated hours.
- Conversion, splits, rule edits, scoped deletions and stops each record one reversible multi-parent receipt. Undo/redo restores original plan identities, rules, exclusions, exception flags and recorded-activity links; conflicting later changes are rejected. All-day occurrence deletion/restoration now captures its series rule too. History's session-storage format advances once to discard incompatible old receipts; saved plans remain intact.
- Migrations `0018_salty_zombie.sql` and `0019_calendar_occurrence_identity.sql` add all-day series metadata and snapshot receipts, advance both parents on membership changes, and preserve nominal occurrence identity while allowing conversion/splitting. Obsolete series undo receipts are discarded. Local development migration succeeded; schema generation reports no remaining changes. The schema has 29 application tables and 20 migrations.
- Verification: workspace typechecks, zero-warning lint/import boundaries, 63 unit tests and six architecture tests passed. The full PostgreSQL suite passed all 89 tests; after the final scoped-edit guard, all 15 Calendar integration tests passed. API and webpack web production builds passed. All six new desktop/phone browser flows passed across the final focused runs, covering conversion with identity-preserving undo/redo, following edits/deletion, stop dates, all-day creation and overrides, move/resize scopes, and lost-response retry with the original key/revision. The all-day rule editor passed axe accessibility. Initial checks caught a scope-selector labeling issue, which was fixed, and corrected test fixtures/resize targeting. Workspace formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. No commit, push or deployment was performed.

## Calendar plan undo/redo and session history, complete

- Owner-authorized scope: one undo/redo system for timed/all-day plans, edits/moves/resizing, individual/group copy-paste, deletion, and whole repeating-series changes. Cmd+Z/Ctrl+Z undoes; Cmd+Shift+Z/Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y redo. Text fields retain native shortcuts. New successful actions clear redo; navigation, selection, copying, failed writes and cancelled drafts do not.
- History is bounded to 100 actions per account/tab. A client store preserves it through Calendar navigation; validated session storage restores it after refresh. Fresh-document visits reset stored history unless the navigation is a refresh, preventing intentional restoration on browser reopen. Sign-out and expired-session redirects clear history. There is no cross-device synchronization, local-storage history, or long-term history feature. Browser session-storage availability controls refresh persistence.
- Each confirmed inverse supplies the next inverse. Existing block-edit and whole-series receipts now return fresh inverse receipts atomically; group/paste undo reuses selection deletion/restoration receipts. Redo retains original identities, details, recurrence exceptions and exclusions, independent of the clipboard. Existing revision/content, ownership, recorded-link and idempotency safeguards remain canonical. No schema migration or dependency was needed.
- Pending history commands persist their original request key before sending. Refresh after a lost successful response offers Retry same change, safely replays the original command, and advances history only after confirmation. Definitive conflicts leave the stack in place. Templates and activity logging retain their earlier visit-only undo behavior; no new redo/persistence was added for those features.
- Focused verification includes unit coverage for bounded stacks, repeated bidirectional revisions, duplicate successful recording, malformed storage, fresh visits, account isolation and pending-request recovery; PostgreSQL coverage for reversible edit/series receipts; and desktop/phone browser flows for navigation/refresh, exact group restoration, changed clipboards, branching, native field shortcuts, sign-out, lost responses and conflicts. Initial browser checks caught incorrect opposite-stack revision advancement, which was corrected and added to the unit regression, plus a test using native macOS Control-click instead of the established Command-click selection gesture.
- Verified: zero-warning lint/import boundaries, workspace typechecks, **61 unit tests and six architecture tests**, **17 focused Planning/Calendar PostgreSQL tests**, both production builds (web with webpack), and **all eight focused desktop/phone browser flows** passed. Formatting and diff whitespace checks passed. The full cross-domain/browser suite was deliberately not rerun for this scoped change. No migration, commit, push or deployment was performed.

## Architecture and completed foundation

The pnpm workspace contains a Next.js web app, an independent Express API, and shared contracts, API client and domain utilities. Backend requests follow routes → services → repositories → Drizzle/PostgreSQL. Only repositories and dedicated infrastructure access persistence; services compose writes through opaque transaction scopes. AST boundary checks enforce those rules. See [architecture](architecture.md) and [foundation decisions](decisions/001-foundation.md).

- Startup verifies database connectivity, schema and restricted runtime permissions before listening. Structured logging, redaction, consistent errors/404s, body limits, readiness, rate limiting, bounded database/HTTP waits and graceful shutdown are implemented. See [foundation operations](foundation.md).
- Authentication uses versioned asynchronous scrypt (N=65536, r=8, p=2), private session DTOs, renewal/revocation, strict endpoint/input controls, exact-origin/Fetch Metadata checks and production cookie protections. Owned reads, writes and references are enforced across domains. See [security](security.md).
- Writes require idempotency keys; edits/deletes require the last-seen revision. Effects and retry receipts commit together. Database checks, composite ownership foreign keys, immutable ownership/history and database-managed revisions reinforce service validation. Exercise/source-time updates are atomic and deduplicated. See [time/history](decisions/002-time-and-history.md) and [write integrity](decisions/003-write-integrity.md).
- Owner policy: application data is disposable. Earlier development/test data was cleared, and legacy password compatibility was removed. No preservation/backfill helpers were introduced. Normal domain operations still keep food snapshots and recorded activity correct.
- Planner, goals, manual food/nutrition, hydration/weight and strength/cardio are implemented. Calendar replaces the separate Dashboard/Week pages. Desktop sidebar and mobile bottom navigation have Calendar, Food, Exercise and Goals; Settings/signout live in the account menu. Exact specification colors are retained. See [UI standards](ui-standards.md).

## Calendar, complete

Day · Week · Month, Week by default, and date navigation stay in the top toolbar. Today and mirrored Add/tools/search actions sit left; Day/Week/Month is centered and previous/date/next sit right. Calendar fills the workspace. Dense Week/Month records remain accessible through the day list; Day shows full overlapping records. Five-minute selection, duration shortcuts, overnight rescheduling, duplicate drafts, date jumping, current-time positioning and scroll memory remain implemented. The removed lower settings/reporting panel stays removed.

The six additions are:

1. Desktop Day/Week drag moves, edge resizing and empty-range draft creation; desktop Month date moves. Touch users enable Move/Resize through the block editor. Snapping, previews, edge scrolling, Escape/blur/pointer cancellation, revision conflicts and retrying an uncertain save are handled. Forms remain available for keyboard and precise editing.
2. Finite daily/weekly repeating plans, individual exceptions/deletions and whole-series edit/delete. Parent locking and revisions prevent competing series/occurrence writes from silently overwriting each other. Recorded activity remains intact when a plan is removed.
3. Day/week copying and reusable templates, with full previews before atomic application. Capture, rename, delete and apply are implemented. Source/preference revisions reject stale previews.
4. All-day and dated/undated unscheduled plans, separate from allocated hours. Scheduling an unscheduled item creates a block and removes the original item atomically; Add a time on an all-day plan creates a separate block and retains the all-day plan.
5. Visible title/type/category filters and owner-scoped, paginated history search with optional date bounds. Filters retain canonical accounting and source-link metadata.
6. Selected-date quick actions reuse Food, Cardio, Strength, Goals, water and weight forms. Every action requires explicit submission.

Focused planning services/repositories and four normalized tables extend the existing architecture. Browser presentation, geometry, interaction state, editors and data access remain separate modules. No dependencies were added. See [Calendar decisions and limits](decisions/004-calendar-expansion.md).

## Food slice, complete

- Food owns a responsive daily log with meal add actions, canonical daily/meal/entry nutrition, and water/weight panels. Narrow layouts offer section links. Date and library selection remain in the URL; corrected entries reveal their saved date.
- Create a missing food from the logging form and return directly to logging. Search recent entries across dates, repeat their original snapshots, edit/delete logs, and archive/restore definitions. Required calories start blank; unknown nutrients stay distinguishable from zero.
- Water supports 250/500 ml shortcuts, custom amounts, daily totals and editable history. Quick logs capture the current instant for today or local noon for other dates; uncertain retries retain the exact payload/key.
- Weight supports preferred-unit input, selected-day records, the latest measurement through that day, a 30-day chart and editable history. Latest/day/chart reads share one database snapshot; later dates are excluded. The chart uses at most 200 actual measurements, explicitly reports truncation, and leaves all records accessible in paginated history.
- Nutrition, Hydration and Body retain separate routes/services/repositories. Reviewed ownership, snapshots, canonical units, daily bounds, revisions, transactions, retry behavior and caches. Corrected literal food search and aligned measurement form validation with API bounds. No dependencies or tables were added. See [Food decisions](decisions/005-food-slice.md).

## Exercise slice, complete

- Strength/Cardio histories now support literal title search and status/type filters. The library supports revision-protected archive/restore; progress shows completed sets from finished workouts and preserves unknown load.
- Workouts support creating an exercise inside the draft, preselected return, repeatable unchecked sets, explicit finish times for past sessions, corrections and confirmed reopening. The responsive save bar sits above mobile navigation; set controls retain keyboard/touch access.
- Saving retains validated set IDs/creation timestamps, keeping ordinary corrections stable across paginated progress. Maximum load and its history page share one read snapshot. Foreign/duplicated child IDs are rejected. Existing snapshots and atomic source-time lifecycle remain intact.
- Generated finish timestamps are captured with the retry key. Lost responses retry the identical command; stale drafts remain editable. Cardio preserves original timestamp precision and displays short durations in seconds. Unchanged saved timestamps remain editable in repeated DST hours; new ambiguous/missing times are rejected.
- Existing services/repositories and shared contracts remain the architecture. The exercise picker and styles are separated from the workout coordinator. Exercise writes invalidate all affected Calendar caches. No dependencies, tables or migration files were added. See [Exercise decisions](decisions/006-exercise-slice.md).

## Admin slice

- Account type is fixed at creation. Public signup creates User accounts; `pnpm admin:create` interactively creates a separate Admin locally, with hidden/confirmed password input and atomic credentials/audit persistence. Existing emails are never converted. PostgreSQL rejects account-type changes and suspended Admin accounts.
- Admin lives in the account menu and has responsive Overview, Users and Audit history sections. Name/email/exact-ID search, status filters and bounded pagination select account metadata only. Confirmed actions suspend/reactivate regular users or revoke all their sessions. Admin accounts cannot be managed through these actions.
- Suspension/session changes use revisions, transactional idempotency receipts and immutable audit entries. Session persistence and domain mutations share account locks with suspension. Login/private requests reject suspended accounts; reactivation requires a new login. Personal endpoints retain ownership checks even for Admin users.
- Statistics report real account/signup/activity counts and aggregate stored Calendar/Food/Exercise/Goals records through public domain services. Activity is updated at most hourly. Health reports database readiness, the API package version, uptime and process-local request/error/abort counters. No personal journal contents are exposed.
- Migration `0010_regular_thing.sql` adds the audit table, fixed account types/status/activity/revisions, indexes and integrity triggers. Local migration succeeded; `pnpm db:generate` reports no schema changes. There are now 24 application tables and eleven tracked migrations. No dependencies, external services, production setup, real Admin account, commit or deployment were added. See [Admin decisions](decisions/007-admin.md).
- Final results: zero-warning lint/import boundaries, formatting, strict workspace typechecks, **43 unit tests, six architecture tests, 68 real-PostgreSQL integration tests and both production builds passed**. The latest aggregate `pnpm verify` passed those stages and **20/21 browser flows**, including Admin on desktop/phone/tablet with WCAG checks. Calendar desktop stopped at a ten-second Month-loading assertion after a transient API 503. With a longer loading wait, the focused `pnpm exec playwright test tests/e2e/calendar-expansion.spec.ts tests/e2e/admin.spec.ts --project=desktop` rerun passed **2/2**. Thus every one of the 21 browser scenarios passed across the aggregate and focused runs; the aggregate command itself was not rerun after this test-only adjustment.
- Earlier browser verification exposed pre-hydration Admin-menu clicks and a Calendar resize starting before its successful retry settled. Tests now wait for loaded/saved state. A separate synthetic concurrency check passed 50 authenticated week reads in five batches of ten. Desktop/phone/tablet Admin screenshots were reviewed and WCAG checks passed. No personal-record access or role mutation was added to resolve testing issues.
- The interactive `pnpm admin:create` command was verified with hidden/confirmed password input against a synthetic test account; its Admin type and scrypt credential were checked, and the account was removed. Frozen offline dependency verification passed using the existing pnpm store; final `git diff --check`, lint and formatting checks passed. Admin setup for the owner remains manual: use a new email with `pnpm admin:create`, sign in normally, then Account menu → Admin. Physical-device Safari and production provisioning remain outside this pass.

## Admin account deletion, complete

- The owner authorized Admin deletion of regular User accounts. Admin → Users now offers Delete account with exact email confirmation, a permanent-data warning and cancellation. Admin accounts, including the acting Admin, remain protected. Escape cannot dismiss a pending or uncertain command and discard its retry handle.
- A thin DELETE route uses shared input contracts, the canonical Identity Admin service and repository. Authorization, target locking, revision and email validation precede deletion. Existing foreign keys cascade private records, credentials and sessions. The immutable audit retains actor/target IDs and a deletion action. Deletion, audit and the acting Admin's idempotency receipt commit together; identical retries work after the target is gone. Audit failure rolls back deletion and cascades.
- Migration `0011_faithful_leper_queen.sql` adds the deletion audit action. Local development and isolated test migrations succeeded; generation reports no further schema changes. There are 24 application tables and twelve tracked migrations. No development accounts were deleted; all deletion verification used disposable synthetic test accounts.
- Verification: strict workspace typechecks, zero-warning lint/import boundaries, **43 unit tests, six architecture tests and all 70 PostgreSQL integration tests passed**. API production build passed; web production build passed using `pnpm --filter @168hours/web exec next build --webpack`. Default Turbopack builds failed because CSS worker processing could not bind a local socket in this environment, including an escalation attempt. No build configuration was changed.
- Admin browser flows passed on desktop, phone and tablet, including cancellation, incorrect/correct email confirmation, dropped committed DELETE responses, identical-key retries and retained audit history. WCAG checks passed, and the phone confirmation screenshot was reviewed. The final browser run passed all three flows with Escape/retry coverage.
- Initial checks caught an accidental extra route argument and a response-union type inference error; both were corrected. Expanded fixtures exceeded the ordinary login rate limit: deletion integration fixtures now use a separate test application, and browser tests omit duplicated login-failure checks retained in integration coverage. Production rate limits were preserved. A synthetic audit error now uses the established unavailable-error SQLSTATE. No commit, push, deployment or production data changes were made.

## Calendar action history, complete

- Replaced the single-deletion handle with a stack of successful timed planned-block actions: create/duplicate, saved title/details/time edits, drag moves, edge resizing and deletion. - Planning owns server-side before/after edit receipts and idempotent revision-protected edit undo. Undo restores exact prior fields and recurrence exception flags. Deletion still restores identity and manual/Cardio/Strength links atomically. Recurring receipts check the series definition, occurrence exclusion and replacement identity, allowing several independent occurrence undos while rejecting changed rules. The client advances earlier expected revisions only after its own confirmed inverse with a matching predecessor; external edits are not overwritten. Same-key retry records/pops each successful action once.
- Scope: history lasts while Calendar remains mounted, including date/view changes. Reloading or leaving Calendar clears it. Date-only plans, whole-series operations, bulk copying/templates, scheduling date-only items, manual sessions and domain activity are outside this first action-history slice. No redo or persisted history UI was added. This supersedes the previous single-delete UI limit.
- Migration 0014 adds `edited_time_blocks` and replaces deletion's parent revision with a series-definition snapshot. Local development/test migrations applied; schema generation reports no remaining changes. Receipts remain owner-scoped and cascade with account deletion. No backfills, production writes, commits, pushes or deployment.

## Calendar time selection and current-hour placement, complete

- A single click on empty Day/Week space selects and highlights a five-minute slot without opening an editor or saving. Toolbar Add opens a one-hour draft at that selected date/time, including a different day in Week. Double-click opens the compact draft directly; dragging still creates a live range. Enter opens a draft from a focused time slot; Space selects it.
- With no time selected, creation uses the current hour in the account timezone on the viewed/requested date. This replaces the fixed 9 AM default in toolbar, Month/date actions, scheduling and clock-change-day creation. No automatic gap search or remembered-time fallback. Overnight drafts retain the next-day end, and invalid/ambiguous clock-change choices remain editable for explicit validation.

## Repeating plans in the plan editor, complete

- New plans now offer Never, Daily, Weekly and Custom repetition in the ordinary compact editor. Weekly offers weekday selection; Custom exposes day/week intervals. Repeat through is required and bounded to one year. The editor shows a plain-language schedule, occurrence count, first dates and skipped clock-change dates before Save. Its unsaved calendar draft follows the first matching occurrence when that differs from the starting day.
- Calendar tools → Add repeating plan opens that same editor with Weekly selected at the chosen calendar date/time. Creation keeps the occurrence-count confirmation. Editing a block now states that it changes one occurrence; the whole-series editor warns that its Save replaces individual edits and previews the resulting dates.

The focused series unit suite passed (3/3), web and API typechecks passed, and changed-file ESLint passed. Calendar activity and expansion browser flows passed on desktop, phone and tablet (6/6), including accessibility checks, creation, series editing, preview and ordinary-plan creation. Initial browser runs found a preview contrast issue, a missing count confirmation and an outdated Other plans assertion; all were corrected and the flows rerun successfully. Formatting and diff whitespace checks followed.

## All-day plans in the ordinary plan editor, complete

- New plans offer All day in the compact editor. It shows one Date by default, with Spans multiple days revealing Last day; timed and repeat controls return when All day is switched off. Calendar tools → Add all-day plan opens this editor with All day selected. Switching a timed draft preserves its title, notes and starting date. The existing 31-day limit and no-hours accounting remain.
- Saved all-day plans have the same date controls. Add a time opens a separate timed draft with the plan's title and notes, leaving the all-day plan in place, including multi-day plans. Unscheduled plans retain their atomic Schedule as time block conversion.
- Web typecheck and changed-file ESLint passed. Calendar activity and expansion browser flows passed on desktop, phone and tablet (6/6), including accessibility checks and all-day/timed coexistence. An initial desktop run exposed an outdated compact-height assertion; a later desktop expansion run intermittently missed an existing block editor after a Month drag, and passed on rerun. Formatting and diff whitespace checks followed.

## Copy, Templates and Routines split, complete

- Calendar tools now separate Copy plans and Templates. Copy reviews and duplicates one selected timed or dated all-day plan, a day, an account-local week or an inclusive range of up to 31 days. The block/item editor offers Copy; relative local dates, clock times, notes and overnight durations carry forward. Recorded activity and recurrence rules do not. Copies remain independent, preserve existing plans and retain source-revision/idempotency checks.
- Templates capture a named day, week or up to 31-day range, including its empty days. Application accepts 1–52 cycles and previews every resulting independent plan. The operation is limited to 100 plans across at most 366 dates; template and preference revisions are checked again on apply. The former “Daily/Weekly routine” labels now say template.
- Routines has its own sidebar page and user-owned API/table. A routine stores 1–50 ordered checklist steps, current checked steps, a completed-run count and last completion time. Create, edit, reorder, check/uncheck, finish and delete are supported. Editing steps clears in-progress checks; finishing requires all steps, increments the count and resets the checklist. All writes use revision/idempotency protections, and private reads and writes are scoped to the signed-in user. Routine steps do not create calendar blocks.
- Migration 0015 adds the Routines table and expands template range storage and day offsets. Local development migration succeeded; test migrations and focused ownership/revision checks passed. A final `pnpm db:generate` reported no schema changes. `pnpm verify` passed: zero-warning lint and architecture boundaries, formatting, workspace typechecks, 53 unit tests, six architecture tests, 79 PostgreSQL integration tests, both production builds, and 42 browser flows across desktop, phone and tablet. Browser coverage includes selected-plan and range copying, multi-cycle templates, routine progress across reload, step reordering and accessibility. A first broad run identified outdated sidebar assertions, a routine completion revision race, a September Exercise fixture that relied on today's date, and the test signup limiter; all were corrected and the full run passed.

## Calendar keyboard copy and date selection, complete

- Timed blocks can be copied with Command-C/Control-C and pasted at a selected five-minute slice with Command-V/Control-V; Month pastes keep the original clock time. Month supports drag range selection, Command/Control-click toggling and Shift-click ranges; the earliest selected date anchors sparse copies, so gaps shift with the selection. Week day headings now select on one click and open Day on double-click. Clipboard state survives calendar navigation in the current browser tab and leaves text-field shortcuts alone.
- Pasting selected days opens the existing plan preview and preserves unrelated destination plans. The sparse-day and target-minute inputs are owner-scoped, validated and bound to the preview token. A single timed-block paste is immediately undoable; a multi-plan paste, including all-day plans, undoes atomically as one Calendar action with ownership and revision checks. Right-click menus remain future work.

Copying a Day with Command-C/Control-C requires selecting that heading; an unselected Day no longer silently replaces the clipboard. - Mouse selection now starts on pointer-down in Month, Week and Day. A Month drag releases its click-suppression state even when the pointer ends on another cell, so the next single click immediately replaces the range; Week headings use the same immediate selection timing. The Month outline follows selected dates, and Command/Control-clicking the last selected date clears its time target as well. Shift and Command/Control selection, drag ranges and Week double-click navigation remain available. - Pasting copied days in Day view requires selecting its day heading, matching Day copy. Timed blocks can target a selected five-minute slice. - Form-based Copy plans now records the created timed and date-only plans as one Calendar undo action, using the same revision-checked copy undo as keyboard paste. Template application remains outside this history. The Copy/Templates browser flow passed on desktop and phone (2/2), including one-step undo of a two-day range, a single timed plan and an all-day plan, plus template application; web typecheck, changed-file ESLint, Prettier and diff whitespace checks passed.
- Individual all-day plans can now be selected with one click and edited with a double-click in Calendar views. Command/Control-C copies the selected plan rather than its whole day; Command/Control-V pastes it on a selected Month day or a selected Day heading, through the existing preview and one-action undo. Selecting an all-day plan clears timed-block selection, and vice versa. Web typecheck, changed-file ESLint, Prettier and diff whitespace checks passed. The Copy/Templates and Calendar expansion browser flows passed on desktop and phone (4/4), as did the existing clipboard flow (2/2); an initial desktop Copy/Templates run sent Paste before the destination Day heading rendered, and passed after the test waited for the heading.

Plain click selects one; Command/Control-click toggles one; Shift-click selects the chronological span from the last anchor, including mixed timed/all-day plans. Paste uses the existing preview and creates one undo action. Delete/Backspace removes a multi-selection atomically and one undo restores the whole group; consecutive undo of Delete followed by Paste also works. Selection copy and group deletion are owner-scoped, revision-checked and limited to 100 plans spanning at most 31 days. Clicking or dragging day headings still supports additive and range selection. - Copy and paste now allow exact overlap with the source and with other plans for single blocks, groups, date ranges and all-day plans. Removed the per-source skip rule, its preview count and its feedback; the copy preview now contains every selected plan. Undo still removes only the newly created copies. The full PostgreSQL integration run passed (80/80 across 20 files), including same-date duplication and all-day overlap; desktop and phone clipboard/multi-selection browser flows passed (4/4), including exact-source copy and undo. API/web typechecks, changed-file ESLint, Prettier and diff whitespace checks passed.
- Day-view paste now also accepts a selected day heading for one timed block or a mixed selection, preserving original clock times like Week and Month. Selecting a five-minute slice still shifts timed blocks to the selected time; without either destination selection, paste prompts for one. An initial run sent Paste before the destination Day grid loaded; the tests now wait for its heading. Web typecheck, changed-file ESLint, Prettier and diff whitespace checks passed.
- Calendar keyboard Copy now captures the selected block, all-day plan, group or selected days into an account-scoped snapshot in the current tab. Paste reads that snapshot rather than refetching the source, so edits, moves and deletion after Copy do not change it; repeated pastes use the same captured data until another Copy. The snapshot paste service validates plan details and owner-scoped references, uses the current account timezone for destination placement, binds the preview to account preferences, and retains the existing one-action undo. A versioned browser-storage key drops old reference-only clipboard data. The Calendar PostgreSQL suite passed (7/7), covering single and mixed snapshot paste after source edits/deletions, account-bound preview tokens, and undo. Desktop and phone clipboard/multi-selection browser flows passed (6/6), including source deletion before paste; the Copy plans form regression passed on both (2/2). API/web typechecks, changed-file ESLint and Prettier, import boundaries, diff whitespace, and API and webpack web production builds passed.
- Keyboard paste now immediately applies captured blocks, all-day plans, groups and selected days through one snapshot path, with no confirmation dialog. Each paste records one undo action; a four-second “Plans pasted · Undo” message offers direct undo while that paste is the latest action, and Command/Control-Z remains available afterward. The separate Copy plans form retains its preview flow. Desktop and phone clipboard, multi-selection and Copy/Templates browser tests passed on the final runs (8/8); one phone Copy plans form undo assertion failed on the first combined run and passed on rerun. Web typecheck, changed-file ESLint and Prettier, import boundaries and diff whitespace passed.

Failed keyboard targets continue to show a short hint, while ordinary creation, edits, navigation and selection stay free of visible notices. Command/Control-Z remains available after the notice closes. Desktop and phone clipboard/multi-selection browser tests passed (6/6), including copy feedback and deletion through the notice Undo button; web typecheck, changed-file ESLint/Prettier and diff whitespace passed.
- Keyboard Copy/Paste failures now use the same notice: missing selections or clipboard content, a calendar still loading, stale or oversized selections, browser storage errors, and paste API errors all give a short explanation. An uncertain paste shows Retry in the notice, reusing the pending idempotency key; its persistent retry control remains available after the notice expires. Successful retries still create one undo action.
